# Source restrictions for this assessment

The canonical complete source has 4434 English automatic-caption events. E labels are one-based; source JSON indices are zero-based. Read the entire source for scope, but only the approved Malpass opening and direct exchange may support judgments. Exact motion: Does suffering provide evidence against theism?

Pro: Alex Malpass — Suffering favors indifference. Con: Randal Rauser — Theistic background weakens the inference. This is an evidential likelihood question. Malpass does not claim logical incompatibility, disproof of God, proof of materialism, or a decisive overall probability. Rauser criticizes the inference when conditioned on richer prior theistic beliefs; he need not prove God exists or deny every bare-theism result. The con route is critical, not an assigned contrary theorem.

## Meaning-changing qualifications

Rauser mentions open/process/imperfect theisms but explicitly returns to classical omnipotence, omniscience and goodness. Do not attribute those alternatives as his adopted escape. Malpass explicitly qualifies his hand-twin/ad-hoc analogy rather than alleging dishonesty. Rauser distinguishes existing background belief from a freshly invented auxiliary. Malpass grants a small amount of biblical support for animal resurrection while disputing its specificity; do not erase that concession. Rauser concedes that the modest argument works against bare theism while disputing its force against richer Christian background. Do not turn this into unqualified agreement with atheism or a decisive defeater. Cognitive closure is defended as limited to divine reasons, not global skepticism. Rauser explicitly says suffering is bad in itself and defends possible goods and fallible personal testimony, not that all victims endorse trauma. Malpass's counterfactual and symmetry challenges remain objections, not proven empirical population correlations. In the final reply Rauser distinguishes his brother's actual causal history from claims about all carers.

# Source restrictions for this assessment

The canonical complete source has 4434 English automatic-caption events. E labels are one-based; source JSON indices are zero-based. Read the entire source for scope, but only the approved Malpass opening and direct exchange may support judgments. Exact motion: Does suffering provide evidence against theism?

Pro: Alex Malpass — Suffering favors indifference. Con: Randal Rauser — Theistic background weakens the inference. This is an evidential likelihood question. Malpass does not claim logical incompatibility, disproof of God, proof of materialism, or a decisive overall probability. Rauser criticizes the inference when conditioned on richer prior theistic beliefs; he need not prove God exists or deny every bare-theism result. The con route is critical, not an assigned contrary theorem.

## Meaning-changing qualifications

Rauser mentions open/process/imperfect theisms but explicitly returns to classical omnipotence, omniscience and goodness. Do not attribute those alternatives as his adopted escape. Malpass explicitly qualifies his hand-twin/ad-hoc analogy rather than alleging dishonesty. Rauser distinguishes existing background belief from a freshly invented auxiliary. Malpass grants a small amount of biblical support for animal resurrection while disputing its specificity; do not erase that concession. Rauser concedes that the modest argument works against bare theism while disputing its force against richer Christian background. Do not turn this into unqualified agreement with atheism or a decisive defeater. Cognitive closure is defended as limited to divine reasons, not global skepticism. Rauser explicitly says suffering is bad in itself and defends possible goods and fallible personal testimony, not that all victims endorse trauma. Malpass's counterfactual and symmetry challenges remain objections, not proven empirical population correlations. In the final reply Rauser distinguishes his brother's actual causal history from claims about all carers.

Excluded Q&A includes further animal-resurrection commitments, an evil-God symmetry challenge, and parent/medical-procedure analogies.
